\Delta_{\bm{Q}^{\prime}}\geq\Delta_{\bm{Q}}/2
. Similarly, the

\displaystyle\bm{Q}\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}[\bm{H}]\bm{Q}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle E_{5}}}{{\approx}}\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}\beta,\tau,\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}}}_{\{\bm{R}^{a}\}}[\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}}]\quad\text{for}\quad\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}}:=\bm{H}\bm{Q}+E_{\bm{Q}}(\bm{I}-\bm{Q})

(L.83)

with
{E_{5}}
also bounded by the RHS of Eq. (
L.82
) but with
\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert\rightarrow 0
.

Now, we may use the monotonicity of gradient (
Theorem

12
) for Hamiltonian pairs
\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}}
and
\bm{H}^{\prime}_{\bm{Q}^{\prime}}
, jumps
\{\bm{R}^{a}\}_{a\in S}
, with the characteristic Bohr-frequency gap
\Delta_{\nu}=\min_{\nu_{1}\neq\nu_{2}\in B(\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}})}|\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}|
.
The modified Hamiltonian perturbation
\|\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}}-\bm{H}^{\prime}_{\bm{Q}^{\prime}}\|
is bounded by the sum of the following two errors:

\left\lVert\bm{H}\bm{Q}-\bm{H}^{\prime}\bm{Q}^{\prime}\right\rVert=\left\lVert\bm{H}\bm{Q}-\bm{H}\bm{Q}^{\prime}+\bm{H}\bm{Q}^{\prime}-\bm{H}^{\prime}\bm{Q}^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq 8\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}}\left\lVert\bm{H}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert\leq 9\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}}\left\lVert\bm{H}\right\rVert,

(L.84)

\displaystyle\left\lVert E_{\bm{Q}}(\bm{I}-\bm{Q})-E^{\prime}_{\bm{Q}^{\prime}}(\bm{I}-\bm{Q}^{\prime})\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert E_{\bm{Q}}(\bm{I}-\bm{Q})-E_{\bm{Q}}(\bm{I}-\bm{Q}^{\prime})+E_{\bm{Q}}(\bm{I}-\bm{Q}^{\prime})-E^{\prime}_{\bm{Q}^{\prime}}(\bm{I}-\bm{Q}^{\prime})\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 8E_{\bm{Q}}\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}}+\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert\leq 9\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}}\left\lVert\bm{H}\right\rVert.

(L.85)

So
\|{\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}}-\bm{H}^{\prime}_{\bm{Q}^{\prime}}}\|\leq 18\|\bm{V}\|\|\bm{H}\|/\Delta_{\bm{Q}}
, which is less than
\Delta_{\nu}/8
by the assumption in the corollary statement so we may apply
Theorem

12
.
Note that error due to perturbing the
\bm{Q}(\bm{I}-\bm{P})
can be bounded directly by

\left\lVert\bm{Q}^{\prime}(\bm{I}-\bm{P}^{\prime})-\bm{Q}(\bm{I}-\bm{P})\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\bm{Q}^{\prime}-\bm{Q}\right\rVert+\left\lVert\bm{P}-\bm{P}^{\prime}\right\rVert=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}}+\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\nu}}\Big).

(L.86)

Apply
Theorem

12
with
\bm{H}=\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}}
,
\bm{H}^{\prime}=\bm{H}^{\prime}_{\bm{Q}^{\prime}}
,
\bm{O}=\bm{Q}(\bm{I}-\bm{P})
, and
\bm{O}^{\prime}=\bm{Q}^{\prime}(\bm{I}-\bm{P}^{\prime})
,
\delta_{\lambda}\leq\|\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}}-\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}^{\prime}}\|
, and
\theta_{\max}=\mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{0})
, along with the additional approximation error in Eq. (
L.82
), we obtain the result as advertised in the corollary statement.
∎

Corollary L.2

(Monotonicity of gradient on a subspace under off-block-diagonal perturbation;
Corollary

H.2
restated)
.

In the setting of Corollary
L.1
, instead assume
\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\nu}},\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}}\leq(const.)
, and that the perturbation is off-block-diagonal, i.e.,
\bm{Q}\bm{V}\bm{Q}=(\bm{I}-\bm{Q})\bm{V}(\bm{I}-\bm{Q})=0
. Then,

\displaystyle-\bm{Q}\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}[\bm{H}]\bm{Q}\succeq r\bm{Q}(\bm{I}-\bm{P})-\epsilon\bm{I}\quad\text{implies}\quad-\bm{Q}^{\prime}\mathcal{L}^{\prime{\dagger}}[\bm{H}^{\prime}]\bm{Q}^{\prime}\succeq r\bm{Q}^{\prime}(\bm{I}-\bm{P}^{\prime})-\epsilon^{\prime}\bm{I}

(L.87)

where

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon+\left|{S}\right|\cdot\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{1}{\tau}+\frac{\left\lVert\bm{H}\right\rVert^{3/4}}{\tau^{1/4}}+\frac{\Lambda_{0}^{2/3}}{\tau^{1/3}}+\frac{\Lambda_{0}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\nu}\tau}}+\frac{\Lambda_{0}}{\sqrt{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}\tau}}+\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\beta\Delta_{\nu}/4}}{\beta}+\frac{\mathrm{e}^{-\beta\Delta_{\bm{Q}}/4}}{\beta}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert^{2}}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}}+\left\lVert\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}}\right\rVert\cdot\Big(\frac{\left\lVert\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}}\right\rVert\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}\Delta_{\nu}}+\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert^{2}}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}\Delta_{\nu}}\Big)+r\Big(\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}}+\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert^{2}}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}\Delta_{\nu}}\Big)\bigg).

(L.88)

Proof.

When the perturbation
\bm{V}
is off-diagonal in the energy eigenbasis of
\bm{H}
, we can use tighter bounds on the changes in eigenvalues and eigensubspaces of
\bm{H}^{\prime}
from
Lemma

L.4
, which implies

\displaystyle\delta_{\lambda}=\max_{j}|E_{j}-E_{j}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert^{2}}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}}\Big)

(originally
\mathcal{O}(\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert)
)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}}-\bm{H}^{\prime}_{\bm{Q}^{\prime}}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\left\lVert\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}}\right\rVert\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}}+\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert^{2}}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}}\right).

(originally
\mathcal{O}(\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert)
)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bm{P}-\bm{P}^{\prime}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}}+\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert^{2}}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}\Delta_{\nu}}\Big).

(originally
\mathcal{O}(\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\nu}})
)

The second line and third line are due to rotation and then subspace perturbation (Lemma
L.3
).
Essentially, this is because (1) all subspace rotations are small (
\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}}\ll 1
) and (2) the energy perturbation is
smaller
than the level spacing (
\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert^{2}}{\Delta_{\bm{Q}}}\ll\Delta_{\nu}
).
We then follow the same argument in the proof of
Corollary

L.1
above with the improved bounds for Eqs. (
L.84
), (
L.85
), and (
L.86
).
We can also use an improve bound of
\theta_{\max}\leq\|\bm{H}_{\bm{Q}}\|
.
Together these improvements yield the better error bound on
\epsilon^{\prime}
as advertised.
∎

L.7
Example where perturbation kills energy gradient

We present an example where despite
\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert\ll\Delta_{\nu}
, the gradient is lost due to the perturbation. Therefore, the resulting change in gradient is not multiplicative
(1-\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\nu}})
, but merely additive. If the gradient is polynomially small, we need
\frac{\left\lVert\bm{V}\right\rVert}{\Delta_{\nu}}
to be also polynomially small to secure the gradient.

Proposition L.7

(Perturbation kills the gradient)
.

Let
\bm{H}=\bm{Z}=\ket{1}\bra{1}-\ket{-1}\bra{-1}
,
\bm{A}=\bm{Z}+\epsilon\bm{X}
, and
\bm{V}=\epsilon\bm{X}
. Then, for the
\beta,\tau\rightarrow\infty
heat bath Lindbladian, we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}[\bm{H}]\preceq-2\epsilon^{2}(\bm{I}-\bm{P})\quad\text{but}\quad\mathcal{L}^{\prime{\dagger}}[\bm{H}^{\prime}]=0.

(L.89)

Proof.

\mathcal{L}^{{\dagger}}[\bm{H}]=\epsilon^{2}\left(\ket{1}\bra{-1}\bm{H}\ket{-1}\bra{1}-\frac{1}{2}\ket{1}\bra{1}\bm{H}-\frac{1}{2}\bm{H}\ket{1}\bra{1}\right)=-2\epsilon^{2}\ket{1}\bra{1}.

(L.90)

But
\mathcal{L}^{\prime{\dagger}}[\bm{H}^{\prime}]=0
because
[\bm{H}^{\prime},\bm{A}]=0
, i.e.,
\bm{A}
is diagonal in the new energy basis.
∎
